The rules now use the `//` absolute spelling, `HIVE_AGENT_CWD` is a second write root in all three grants (exported before the permission flags resolve, degrading gracefully to workspace-only when unset), and the pinned test asserts the exact rule spelling so an inert-rule regression fails in CI rather than in a contributor's pane.
- The exit-77 documentation now matches what the entrypoint actually does since the #6003 netfilter-module preflight ([#6223](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6223)). `manual-provisioning.md`, the standalone overlay README, and the Podman rootful/rootless baseline docs still said exit 77 meant exactly "grant `CAP_NET_ADMIN`" and that every other cause exited 1, so an operator on a node missing `xt_mark` or `xt_REDIRECT` would grant the capability and keep crashlooping. They now describe both causes, the log lines that tell them apart (the module case names the module and never prints `Grant NET_ADMIN`), the node-side `/etc/modules-load.d/` fix, and `net-admin-requirement.md` gains the full check order (binary selection, preflight, real ruleset, fail-closed decision) with which tests and CI lanes exercise each cause.
- Each per-UID agent now gets its own `XDG_DATA_HOME` and `XDG_STATE_HOME` (`/data/home/agents/<agent>/.local/share` and `.../.local/state`) instead of every agent's `~/.local` being a symlink into the one shared `/data/home/.local` ([#6238](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6238)). Backend CLIs keep their session transcripts, run locks, sqlite state and plugin caches there, so the shared tree was a contended resource owned by whichever agent wrote first, held together only by the Dockerfile umask wrappers - a per-CLI allowlist that failed closed for every new backend (muse crash-looped hundreds of times before it was added) and that CLIs which check their state root's permissions refused to trust. The launch environment now exports both variables explicitly, following the per-agent `CODEX_HOME` precedent, and provisioning makes `~/.local` a real agent-owned directory, retiring a legacy symlink bridge when it finds one. Sharing stays explicit and narrow: `~/.config` (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, holding gh and goose config) and the credential directories bridged by `SharedAuth` (`.claude`, `.gemini`, `.codex`, `.copilot`, `.bob`) are unchanged, and `~/.local/share/opencode` (where `opencode auth login` writes its credential) is bridged back to the shared tree so one login still reaches the fleet. Nothing is migrated: existing state under the shared tree is left where it is and agents start fresh per-agent state on their next session. `HIVE_SHARED_AGENT_HOME=1` keeps the legacy layout whole.
- Commits agents author as this hive's GitHub App bot now pass the DCO check ([#6251](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6251), [#6254](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6254)). The derived git identity used GitHub's own bot address, `<slug>[bot]@users.noreply.github.com`, and probot-dco validates the sign-off email's syntax before comparing it to the author: the `[` and `]` in the local-part fail that check, so every such commit was rejected as "not a valid email address" - a matching `Signed-off-by` could not fix it, a second human sign-off could not fix it, and with no `.github/dco.yml` there was no remediation path (seen on [#6164](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/pull/6164), which had to be re-authored by hand). The bot identity keeps its `<slug>[bot]` name but its address is now the bracket-free `<slug>@hive.kubestellar.io` (domain configurable via `HIVE_GIT_BOT_EMAIL_DOMAIN`), the same shape as the agent PRs that already pass DCO; because `git commit -s` writes that same address into the trailer, author and sign-off match. The App still owns the PR and the push. A `project.ai_author` naming a user keeps its attributable `<login>@users.noreply.github.com` address, which is already valid, and hives without a usable App are unchanged. The entrypoint regression test now asserts that every derived identity is a syntactically valid, bracket-free address.

### Security

- The merge-request watcher no longer merges a PR without independently verifying CI, and absent check runs are no longer treated as passing ([#6173](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6173)). `handleOneMergeRequest` used to call `MergePR` unconditionally and relied on GitHub's branch protection to refuse a red PR; on a base branch with no protection rules nothing refused it, and a fork PR whose four workflows had all died at startup with zero jobs (so zero check runs and an empty status rollup) was merged 13 minutes after opening. The watcher now requires positive confirmation on the pinned head SHA before any merge: at least one commit status or check run exists, every gating one concluded success (`neutral`/`skipped` accepted, the existing non-required ignore list and `auto_merge.required_checks` set still apply), every config-declared required check has actually reported, and no workflow run on the SHA failed without producing a job. Zero evidence is refused as unverified; queued/in-progress CI parks the request (`ci_waits` in the result file, no retry consumed) for up to one hour; a red verdict is a failed attempt that re-engages the fix loop exactly like a branch-protection refusal; an API error while gathering evidence is a failed attempt, never a pass. The self-merge sweep's `commitGreen` is unchanged in behavior and now shares its evaluation core with the gate. This is the v4 port of [#6279](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/pull/6279).

## 2026-09-08 (v4.18.4)

### Fixed

- CI now reads the Go toolchain version from `src/go.mod` (`go-version-file`) instead of hard-coding `go-version: '1.25'` in every `setup-go` step. Twelve pins across `coverage-hourly.yml`, `go-security-analysis.yml`, `testutil-guard.yml`, `v2-ci.yml`, and `v2-tests.yml` had drifted below what dependency bumps require, so every dependabot PR whose module declared `go 1.26` (for example golang.org/x/sys 0.48.0 in #6253) failed at load with `go.mod requires go >= 1.26.0` rather than on the code under test. Bumping the module's `go` directive now propagates to CI automatically ([#6260](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6260)).
- The `go` directive in `src/go.mod` moves from `1.25.6` to `1.25.14`. Reading the toolchain from go.mod means CI now uses exactly the pinned patch level instead of "latest 1.25.x", and 1.25.6 carries three stdlib advisories govulncheck flags (GO-2026-6090, GO-2026-6091, GO-2026-6218 in `net/url` and `html/template`). 1.25.14 is the current patched release.

## 2026-09-08 (v4.18.3)

### Changed

- The Linear work-tracker section of every kick now tells agents two things it left out before. First, which credential to use when both are present: `LINEAR_ACCESS_TOKEN` (the connected Hive app's own grant, so writes are authored by the Hive app user) always wins over `LINEAR_API_KEY` (a personal key that attributes writes to its human owner) — agents given both had been picking the personal key, and every issue they filed showed the operator as its creator. Second, how to label: a bare `issueCreate` lands unlabeled, so the section now shows the `issueLabels` lookup, `labelIds` and `priority` on create, and `issueAddLabel` after, mapping whatever labels the policy applies to GitHub issues onto Linear labels of the same names.

### Fixed

- Automated releases no longer strand ~8 spurious zero-job `failure` runs per release ([#6084](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6084)). `tagged-release.yml` opens a PR from the `release-gate/v<VERSION>` scratch branch into `v4`, merges it within seconds, and its cleanup trap deleted the scratch branch immediately - while the pull_request-triggered workflows fired by the PR open (Changelog Reminder, Docs Index Reminder, Build and Push Docker Image, changelog-fragment-guard, Copilot DCO, ...) were still queued. With the head ref gone they zombied out as `conclusion: failure` with an empty jobs list after 10-45 minutes; three releases on one day produced ~24 such runs, which dominated `gh run list --status failure` and masked real regressions. The trap now cancels every `queued`/`in_progress` run on that scratch branch (REST `actions/runs?branch=release-gate/v<VERSION>` then `POST .../cancel`) before deleting it, so stranded runs conclude `cancelled` - the accurate verdict. The cancellation is best-effort (`|| true` throughout, it can never fail a release) and scoped strictly to the ephemeral scratch ref this workflow owns.
- The agent permissions watcher's tick loop now takes its lifetime and scan interval as parameters ([#6174](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/pull/6174)). `StartPermissionsWatcher` is unchanged for callers and still runs for the life of the process on the constant `PermissionFixInterval`, but it now delegates to an inner `runPermissionsWatcher(ctx, logger, interval)` that returns when its context is cancelled. That replaces a package-level mutable interval and stop channel that a first cut of the lifecycle test had introduced: with globals, the test could not know when the goroutine had actually exited before restoring `WatchedHomeDirs`/`GooseLogsDir`, and the race detector caught the watcher reading those paths while cleanup rewrote them. The tests now cancel the context and join the goroutine before touching any shared seam, with no sleeps standing in for synchronization, and cover the watcher creating its directories on startup, repairing a broken mode file on a tick, and stopping cleanly.
- `bin/test_git_credential_hive.sh` no longer tests the live host instead of itself on per-UID hives. Run as an agent UID (>= 2001), the credential helper derives the agent name from the uid-map — deliberately ignoring the `HIVE_AGENT` the harness sets — and reads that real agent's live `/tmp/.hive-mode-<agent>`, so the two mode-notice assertions failed (or passed vacuously) with the host's deployment state. The harness now stubs `id -u` to a sub-2001 UID so the helper takes the env-var path the harness controls; the helper's own uid-map trust model is untouched.
- The dashboard's **Sign in with GitHub** overlay completes again ([#6216](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6216)). [#5748](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5748) made `flow_id` mandatory on `/api/gh-user-auth/poll` — the binding that stops an anonymous poller racing the operator for a freshly approved session — and taught the standalone login page and `hivectl login` to send it, but missed the in-page overlay in `static/index.html`. That overlay kept polling with an empty body, so every poll was refused with 400 and the login could never complete: GitHub reported the device activated while the dashboard sat on "Waiting for authorization..." indefinitely, with no error. It affected only hives where the dashboard page loads before you sign in (an open hive, or one already carrying `HIVE_DASHBOARD_TOKEN`) — everyone else got the standalone login page, which was never broken, which is why this survived from the 2026-09-02 merge through v4.18.1. The overlay now keeps the `flow_id` from `/start` and presents it on every poll. It also stops on a response it does not recognise and shows the error, instead of silently looping: the 400 body carried `{"ok":false,"error":...}` with no `status` field, so it matched none of the overlay's branches and the timer just fired again — an eternal spinner rather than a message. A regression guard now asserts the page's poll actually carries the id, which is the check that would have caught the original miss; the server-side tests could not, because the browser was the thing being correctly rejected.
- The spoke dashboard (`/api/status`, SSE, and the agent card) now escalates a crash-looping agent to `structuredStatus: BLOCKED` with the rolling-24h restart count and the last crash reason, instead of reporting the same `state=running / busy=working` as an agent doing real work. A live spoke hid a total seven-agent outage (restart_count 918, empty panes, zero transcripts) behind an all-green dashboard for hours; the escalation trips at the same threshold the hub's fleet verdict already uses (`HIVE_HUB_AGENT_RESTART_PROBLEM_THRESHOLD`, default 5), so the local card and the fleet view can no longer disagree about the same agent ([#6237](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6237)).
- Codex agents no longer receive Ctrl+C before each kick, which could exit the CLI and send the quality policy and task into Bash as shell commands. Input clearing now uses Ctrl+U for Codex, including runtime backend overrides.
- Agents no longer see `git-credential-hive.sh: line N: /var/run/hive-metrics/token-access.jsonl: Permission denied` on every clone, fetch, and push. The credential helper appended to the token-access audit log with `>> f 2>/dev/null`, which only mutes the command, not a failed redirection — and the redirection always failed for per-UID agents because the metrics directory is deliberately not agent-writable and the log file was never created. The helper now wraps the append the way `gh-wrapper.sh` already does (#4043) so a failure is silent, and the entrypoint pre-creates the log as `dev:node` mode 0664 so the appends from both writers actually land and `GET /api/token-access` stops reporting an empty audit log on per-UID hives.
- The fix-loop circuit breaker no longer re-posts its "Fix loop escalated — human attention needed" comment on a PR that has already been escalated. The ledger entry for a red PR — including its *escalated* marker — was deleted on any pass that did not see CI as `failure`, and `EnrichCIStatus` reports `pending` for a check-run fetch that *errored* as well as for checks that are still running. One transient GitHub API error therefore re-armed the breaker, and the next red pass burned a fresh re-engagement budget and escalated again: on one hive, a single PR collected fifteen identical escalation comments in 32 hours and another twenty-five. Three changes close this. A `pending` observation now leaves the ledger untouched (it is no information, not a green), and an entry that merely drops out of one enumeration pass is kept for 24h before it is pruned. The `needs-human` label is now the authoritative record of escalation in both directions: a PR that already wears it is treated as escalated even by a freshly-wiped ledger, so the comment can never be posted twice, and a PR whose confirmed label a human later *removes* is genuinely returned to the automated lane (fresh distinct-SHA count and re-engagement budget) — previously the ledger's own flag kept it hands-off forever, so the comment's "remove the label to return the PR to the automated fix lane" instruction did nothing. A label call that fails at escalation time is retried on later passes without re-commenting, with a 5-minute grace so a stale listing right after the label write is not read as a removal.
- Escalation triggered by an exhausted re-engagement budget on a head that never moved now says so ("stayed red on the same commit through N automated fix re-dispatches with no new commit pushed") instead of the misleading "1 distinct fix attempts".
- Dependency-bot PRs (`renovate[bot]`, `dependabot[bot]`, `mergeraptor[bot]`) are no longer treated as hive-agent PRs by the fix-loop machinery. The agent-author test was "configured `ai_author`, or any login ending in `[bot]`", which swept every red Renovate/Dependabot bump into the staleness clock, the reaper, and human escalation — paging a human about crate bumps that no agent had ever touched. The list mirrors the hub's default `hub.contribute_deny_authors`, and the shared `isHiveAgentAuthor` check also recognises the App identity from `EffectiveAIAuthor()`.
- Agents now commit as this hive's own GitHub App bot instead of a hardcoded `kubestellar-hive <hive-bot@kubestellar.io>`. The entrypoint wrote that public identity into `/etc/gitconfig` (and the dev user's global config) on every hive, so a self-hosted hive with its own App authored commits nobody on GitHub could attribute, and rulesets that require attributed changes or a recognised author blocked them — while each agent improvised a different fix per kick (one set the App login by hand, the next committed as `hive-quality <quality@hive.local>`, the next as the hardcoded default). Both config layers now derive the identity the way `Config.EffectiveAIAuthor()` does: `project.ai_author` wins, otherwise a usable App's `<app_slug>[bot]`, otherwise the legacy pair — with the matching `<login>@users.noreply.github.com` address GitHub attributes to the bot. Hives that never opted in, or have no installed App, are unchanged.
- Agent writes to Linear no longer fail with `401 AUTHENTICATION_ERROR` the day after the connected app's OAuth token rotates. Hive refreshes the token and re-pushes it into each agent's tmux session environment hourly, but a running CLI keeps the environment it was forked with, so from the first rotation after launch every Linear call from inside the pane carried a dead token — observed for two days on a live hive, during which the agent had silently fallen back to the operator's personal `LINEAR_API_KEY` and every issue it filed showed the operator as its creator. The egress proxy, which already terminates every agent request to `api.linear.app` for the tier gate, now replaces the `Authorization` header of every ISSUES_ONLY+ request with the hive's current credential (the same resolver the session-env push uses), so the copy in the agent's environment — stale, absent, or a placeholder — no longer matters. Requests below the ISSUES_ONLY floor and requests the gate refuses are untouched.
- Kick messages no longer lose a 400-character chunk when that chunk happens to start with `-`. Kicks are typed into the agent's tmux pane as 400-rune `send-keys -l` slices; without an end-of-options `--` marker tmux read a slice beginning with a hyphen (a boundary landing just before the `-` in `onboard-ai-platform`, `hold-gated`, `[ONB-2651]`, or a markdown bullet) as a flag, rejected the command, and the slice silently vanished — the error was discarded. Observed on a live hive as a held-PR snapshot that ended mid-word and jumped into a later paragraph, on which the quality agent stood down kick after kick. `SendLiteral` now passes `--` before the text and logs a failed send at WARN instead of dropping it.

### Security

- Credentials the hive process holds in its own environment no longer reach agent panes. Every per-agent tmux server inherits the hive's environment into its global environment, and the creation-time strip used `set-environment -u`, which only deletes the session entry and lets the global value show through — so the Linear work-source key (`LINEAR_API_KEY`, the operator's personal key), the Linear OAuth app's `LINEAR_CLIENT_SECRET` and `LINEAR_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, and the full installation token `HIVE_GITHUB_TOKEN` were present in every agent shell. Observed: an agent holding the sanctioned OAuth `LINEAR_ACCESS_TOKEN` used the personal key instead, so every issue it filed was created by the operator rather than by the Hive app user. Session creation now records a `-r` removal for each inherited credential before setting the tier's sanctioned pair, and the downgrade refresh tick removes with `-r` too; a real-tmux regression test forks a pane and asserts what it sees.

## 2026-09-08 (v4.18.2)

### Fixed

- The advisory digest no longer spends a top-N slot on a finding the reporting agent has already withdrawn ([#2364](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/2364)). The 2026-09-05 digest held one of its five CRITICAL slots with `heartbeatBearerOK per-hive binding removed without covering test`, whose own detail begins *"REVISED: ... not removed ... The change is refactoring, not a security regression. Downgrading priority."* — while 282 findings went unshown. "Downgrading priority" never reached the bead: the agent edited the notes, severity is read from the bead's priority, so the finding kept rendering at critical and winning a slot every cycle. A withdrawn finding now ranks below every finding nobody withdrew, in any severity band, and renders with a caption saying the bead wants closing rather than fixing. This is the only demotion that crosses severity bands: [#5945](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5945)'s three signals all mean "nobody re-checked this", so an unverified critical may still be the worst thing in the report, whereas a retraction is the reporting agent re-checking and saying the finding does not stand. It is demoted rather than dropped, because the bead is still open and only a maintainer closes it. Detection requires two signals — a revision marker at the start of the detail AND a phrase saying the finding no longer stands — because "REVISED:" alone is equally how an agent raises a finding's severity, and demoting that would bury the most urgent item in the report.
- Podman/Quadlet deployments no longer keep serving the gateway config they were installed with ([#6078](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6078)). A Quadlet install copies repo files onto the host and runs from there — `nginx.conf` is bind-mounted into the gateway, and the four Quadlet units and two boot units are read from the host — but updating Hive replaces only the *image*, so a fix to `src/deploy/nginx.conf` shipped, released, and never reached a deployment that was already running. Measured: the WebSocket-upgrade fix in [#5200](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5200) merged on 2026-08-30, and a host installed on 2026-08-22 was still rejecting the contributor handshake with HTTP 400 six days later, while `/api/contribute/status` reported a `served_sha` that *contained* the fix — because that SHA describes the binary, not the gateway. `bin/hive-podman-update.sh reconcile` now compares the eight repo-owned files against a checkout, and `reconcile apply` re-copies the stale ones; `hive.yaml`, `hive.env` and `secrets/` are never touched, which is why this exists rather than `setup --force` — that path regenerates `hive.env` and takes `HIVE_DASHBOARD_TOKEN` with it. `pin` now carries the gateway config forward with the image it ships beside, and `status` reports drift unasked, which is the only cover available to the `podman auto-update` path — a systemd timer calling podman directly, with no script in it to hook. `reconcile` exits 78 on drift so a monitor has something to read: a frozen host was otherwise byte-identical to a current one, which is also how a `hive.container` still naming the retired `ghcr.io/kubestellar/hive` path reports `UPDATED=false`, exit 0 and a green timer while updating nothing. **Existing deployments need one manual run** — this fix ships inside the very files that were never refreshed, so it cannot reach them on its own: from a checkout, run `bin/hive-podman-update.sh reconcile apply` (add `--rootful` for a system install), then restart `hive.service` if it reports a rewritten unit.
- The advisory digest no longer re-emits findings computed at an older commit as counted, severity-ranked open items when the finding itself names work that has since closed ([#6080](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6080)). In the 2026-09-05 digest two findings were carried forward this way; one was listed open at the exact commit that fixed it, and the other named its own remediation in its own body — an issue and a PR that had been closed and merged roughly 23.5 hours earlier. Both were holding top-10 HIGH slots while other findings went unshown. The digest now asks GitHub whether the work a stale finding names has closed, and moves the finding to Recently Resolved instead of listing it as open. It retires a finding only when every reference it names resolves as closed — a still-open reference or a lookup that fails keeps it open, and a finding computed AT the analyzed commit is never retired, since a report that an issue was closed too early is exactly what a maintainer needs to see. How long ago that work closed decides only whether the retirement is still announced under Recently Resolved, never whether the finding is open: a retirement that expired after 30 days would return the finding to the counted, severity-ranked list permanently, with nothing about it or the work it names having changed. The GitHub lookups are memoized per digest build and capped, so a large digest cannot spend hundreds of sequential calls re-asking about the same issue — and because a rate-limited lookup keeps a finding open, that is also what stops retirement from quietly failing on exactly the digests big enough to need it. The check runs before the top-N cap, so the freed slot goes to a live finding rather than being spent. Cross-repo references in the digest also linked to a `github.com/gh-<owner>` that does not exist: advisory beads carry an external ref of the form `gh-<owner>/<repo>#<n>`, and the `gh-` source prefix was read as part of the owner, so every one of those links was dead. The renderer now strips the prefix, which heals beads already stored that way, and new beads are written without it — the dedup accepts both forms so no duplicate advisory is opened across the change.
- Contributor task prompts no longer tell an agent to base its work on the branch the *hive binary* was built from when the issue is in someone else's repository ([#6081](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6081)). `taskBaseBranch` passed `upstreamBranch()` through for every task, so a self-hosted hive built from `v4` stamped `v4` onto 100% of its tasks — observed on a hive dispatching for four repositories whose default branch is `main`, none of which has a `v4` at all. That case was loud only because a missing branch fails visibly; a repository that happens to have a `v4` which is not its default would have resolved, opened the PR against it, and satisfied the prompt's own "confirm the base" step — wrong base, no error anywhere. The hive's build branch is now inherited only when the task's repository is the hive's own; every other task gets the existing instruction to resolve that repository's `defaultBranchRef`, which until now was unreachable code. A release-line title (`[v5] ...`) still overrides both, since that is a statement about the work rather than the repository. Because that fallback wording is now the common path rather than dead code, it also gained the operational detail the named-branch wording already had — `git fetch upstream`, an explicit `git checkout -b` from the resolved branch, and a confirm-the-base step — so the half of [#5729](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5729) that matters (never trust the branch the reused checkout is sitting on) holds either way.
- CI runner labels are now a repository variable with a GitHub-hosted default, so a fork of this repository can run its own CI ([#6083](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6083)). 24 `runs-on:` declarations across 12 workflows named the `[self-hosted, hive]` fleet directly, which only `hivecommons` has. In a fork those jobs did not fail — they queued forever, held their workflow's concurrency group so the next push queued behind them, and could not be cancelled, because there is no runner to deliver a cancellation to. Measured on a fork of `v4`: one pull request produced 26 runs, 14 still `queued` an hour later, and a cancel request returned `202 Accepted` while the run stayed `queued` 22 minutes on. Every such `runs-on:` now reads `fromJSON(vars.HIVE_RUNNER_LABELS || '["ubuntu-latest"]')`, so a fork that sets nothing runs on GitHub-hosted runners and a fork with its own fleet can point at it. Each job keeps whatever fork-PR guard it already had — only the label source changed. **`hivecommons` must set `HIVE_RUNNER_LABELS` to `["self-hosted","hive"]` at the org or repo level**, or the heavy lanes move to `ubuntu-latest` on merge; `src/docs/ci-runner-labels.md` carries the command, how to clear runs already wedged, and why the fallback sits inside `fromJSON`'s argument rather than relying on `&&` short-circuiting. `src/deploy/test_runner_labels_portable.sh` fails CI if a `runs-on:` names the fleet directly or drops that fallback.
- **PR Verifier no longer fails every open PR when its own image is unreachable** (#6085). The workflow treated the verification itself as advisory (`continue-on-error`) but the GHCR login and image pull as fatal, so any registry-side change — the kubestellar→hivecommons org move left 11 consecutive runs red on `manifest unknown` — failed the check on every PR at once before any PR content was examined. Login and pull are now advisory too, the verifier step runs only when the pull succeeded, and a skipped verification announces itself with a workflow warning naming the unreachable image, so the check reports on PR contents or says plainly that it could not. `src/deploy/test_pr_verifier_advisory.sh` pins the relative invariant — registry access is never treated more harshly than the verification it enables — which permits either coherent posture and forbids only the split that was there. It is a static check on purpose: the workflow runs on `pull_request_target`, so a PR that fixes this executes the base branch's copy and cannot show itself passing.
- **backend-smoke's pinned lane now tests the repository's own contributor image, not upstream's** (#6086). The lane exists to run the checkout's suite against the CLI binaries the contributor image actually ships, but it hard-coded `ghcr.io/hivecommons/hive-contributor:latest`, so a fork's lane silently exercised upstream's image and stayed green no matter what the fork shipped — while `docker.yml` already derives its push targets from `github.repository_owner`, meaning a fork published one image and tested another. The pull step now derives the image from the repository owner, falls back to the upstream image with a workflow warning when the owner-scoped one is not pullable (a fork that has not published yet still gets a meaningful run), and logs which image the lane pinned either way. The owner is lowercased before it becomes an image reference: container reference paths are lowercase-only, so a fork under an org like `Tuna-OS` would otherwise build an invalid reference that podman rejects before contacting any registry — taking the upstream fallback on every run, which is the same defect by a different route. `bin/test_backend_smoke_image_owner.sh` extracts the selection step from the workflow and runs it against a stubbed podman, so the fork case, the loud fallback, the lowercasing, and "no image at all is a hard failure" are all pinned.
- **Go and other compiled builds now work in `just contribute-hive claude local`** ([#6100](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6100)). The local-mode OS sandbox confines writes to `HIVE_WORKSPACE_DIR`, and every compiled toolchain writes its cache under `$HOME` — `GOCACHE` at `~/.cache/go-build`, `ccache`, `$TMPDIR` — so `go build`, `go test` and `go vet` failed on a hive contributor task, in hive's own Go repo, with an error naming a cache file rather than a sandbox boundary. Observed in 8 separate sessions over 2026-08-31 to 2026-09-05 on one host, each rediscovering the problem and inventing its own `GOCACHE` redirect because nothing told it to. The launcher now creates one hive-owned cache root (`${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}/hive/agent-build`), exports `GOCACHE`, `GOMODCACHE`, `GOTMPDIR`, `TMPDIR` and `CCACHE_DIR` into it on the agent's launch line, and the sandbox grants it as a write root — strictly narrower than widening the sandbox, since it is one directory hive owns rather than `$HOME`. It is a write root **only**: deliberately not in `permissions.allow` and not an `--add-dir`, because Bash is what compilers run under, an agent has no reason to `Edit` a build cache, and adding it as a working directory would put megabytes of object files in the agent's view of the tree. Callers that do not set the variable — every non-local path — keep the workspace as their only write root, and both directions are pinned by tests.
- **Agents stalled on the new "Response stalled mid-stream" Claude Code error are nudged again** — Claude Code renamed the cut-off-mid-stream failure from "Connection lost mid-response" (#4697) to "API Error: Response stalled mid-stream. The response above may be incomplete.", and the new wording carries no HTTP status, so nothing in `transientAPIErrorPatterns` matched and an agent showing it sat idle at `❯` until the next scheduled kick. `stalled mid-stream` is now in the transient-error allowlist, so the manager retries the nudge immediately — same failure, same remedy (#6134).
- Removed the dead `dashboard.snapshot_dir` row from `hive.yaml.example` and the four shipped deploy templates ([#6192](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6192)). The key was parsed into `DashboardConfig.SnapshotDir` but never read — nous baseline snapshots hardcode `/data/nous/snapshots`, and the public `/snapshot` page is driven by `hub.auto_snapshot` — so operators tuning it were changing nothing. The unused Go field is left for a code-side follow-up.
- The contributor activity stream (`/api/contribute/events`) now tells a client when it missed events ([#6218](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6218)). A subscriber whose channel fills has events discarded rather than back-pressuring the hub — that is deliberate and unchanged, since a slow observer must never stall worker assignment — but the loss was invisible: the connection stayed open, the 25-second heartbeat kept it looking healthy, and a long-lived client could not tell "nothing happened" from "I missed events". For an unattended monitor that is the dangerous failure, because stale data renders as current until some unrelated reconnect repairs it. Two additive fields fix it. Every frame now carries `seq`, a monotonic stream position assigned once at fan-out so the same event has the same number for every subscriber — a jump from 41 to 45 means three events never arrived, and the `hello` frame reports the position the client connected at so it has a baseline. And when events are discarded for a connection, the server sends a `gap` frame naming how many went and where the stream has reached. The connection stays open and reconnect-with-replay is still the recovery path; what changed is that a client now knows to take it. The gap is reported before the next activity frame, and on the heartbeat tick if none arrives, so a stream that has gone quiet still surfaces it rather than sitting on pings forever. Existing clients ignore both fields and are unaffected; the `/contribute` page uses the signal to re-sync immediately instead of waiting out its poll timer.
- Removed duplicate `TestProviderErrorStatusClass`, `TestPaneAfterKickBaseline` and `TestClearProviderErrorLocked` declarations in `pkg/agent` that landed via #6107 and #6151 and broke `go vet` / `golangci-lint` on v4.
- The image publisher (`src/scripts/publish-image-tags.sh`) now retries transient registry read failures (3 attempts) around its `docker buildx imagetools inspect` calls instead of failing the whole publish on a single TCP reset. Run 33949672991 went red on the v4 merge lane when a post-publish verification read hit a GHCR CDN `connection reset by peer` after the tags had already been published. A definitive `manifest unknown` answer still resolves immediately, and persistent registry failures still fail closed.
- Agent-opened PRs can no longer go out with an empty body. `hive-open-pr` used to silently drop `--body-file` (a flag `gh pr create` accepts and agents routinely use), so the request carried an empty body and the opened PR's only content was the attribution footer — observed repeatedly on quality/sec-check PRs. The script now honors `--body-file`/`-F` (and the gh short flags `-R/-H/-B/-t/-b`), warns on any other flag it drops, and refuses an empty body loudly; the PR-request watcher independently rejects an empty body at the server-side choke point, so no client can open a body-less PR.
- Agent PRs that resolve an issue now close it on merge. The policy templates and kick instructions hedged closing keywords ("`Fixes #N` ONLY if this fully resolves it — use `Refs #N` instead for an epic/multi-phase tracker"), which agents read cautiously enough that about half of resolving PRs shipped a non-closing `Refs #N` and left their issue open after the fix landed. The guidance now makes `Closes #N` the stated normal case and confines `Refs #N` to a deliberately-partial fix that must say, on the same line, what is left open and why. A new `hive-open-pr --issues <N>` declares the originating issue, and the watcher rejects a request whose body no longer references each declared issue — so a truncated body cannot open a PR that orphans its issue.
- The hive's own "🐝 Hive Advisory Report" and bot dependency dashboards (Renovate's "Dependency Dashboard", Dependabot's equivalent) no longer count as actionable issues. Both are standing meta issues — a report the hive files itself and machine-maintained control panels — so they inflated every repo's Actionable count permanently and were fed into agent kick prompts, where an agent "working" a Renovate dashboard can tick checkboxes that instruct Renovate to act. The contribute queue already denied both to human contributors by default; the agent path now applies the same judgment, structurally, at the same enumeration choice point as the hold/exempt checks. Real issues that merely mention dependencies stay actionable: the dashboard skip requires a known bot author AND a dashboard title.

## 2026-09-05 (v4.18.1)

### Security

- The contributor relay's `tmuxSendNudge()` now passes its message to `tmux send-keys -l` as a literal argv element instead of interpolating it into naked single quotes in a shell command ([#6069](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6069)). Both current callers pass vetted quote-free constants, so this was not exploitable today — but a single apostrophe added to a future nudge message would have escaped the quoting and executed as shell inside the contributor container. A regression test now sends a hostile message through the nudge path and pins that it arrives as one literal argv element.

## 2026-09-05 (v4.18.0)

### Added

- The hub now detects when two spokes manage the same repo ([#5691](https://github.com/kubestellar/hive/issues/5691)). Splitting one large hive into several subject-scoped spokes exists so agents stop competing over unrelated work — but nothing stopped two spokes claiming the same repo, which puts two fleets on one backlog opening competing PRs on it, the exact failure the split prevents. The hub already received every spoke's repo list on every heartbeat and never compared them; reported live while splitting a 43-repo hive in two, where an org watcher read one spoke's repo list, could not read the other's, concluded 18 repos were unmanaged, and was one step from adding them to the first hive. `GET /api/registry` now carries a `repoOverlaps` array — omitted entirely when the fleet is clean, so its presence is the signal — and a **warning** fleet alert (`repo-overlap`) is raised on *both* hives in a pair, naming the other spoke and the shared repos. Matching resolves bare (`docs`), owner-qualified (`tunaos/docs`) and URL spellings to one identity and is case-insensitive, because two separately-configured spokes are exactly the pair most likely to spell one repo differently; the GitHub host is part of that identity, so the same `owner/repo` on github.com and on an enterprise instance is correctly *not* a conflict, and a spoke listing its own primary repo in `repos[]` is one claim rather than a conflict with itself. This is read-side only: derived per request from data the registry already holds, adding nothing to the heartbeat protocol and persisting nothing, which keeps the hub a directory rather than a control plane. It reports and does not refuse — a repo two subject areas legitimately share is a real case, so the alert is acknowledgeable like any other, and whether an overlap should ever *block* assignment remains open on the RFC.

- The quality lane can now opt into agent-authored formal verification with `quality.formal: true` at ACMM L5/L6. Every quality kick receives the same model-worthiness, `formal/<subsystem>/` artifact, expected-verdict drift, reporting-only CI, counterexample narrative/deduplication, and modeled-subsystem maintenance contract even when its ordinary policy prompt is customized. The zero value is off, and the setting becomes inert on an ACMM downgrade below L5 without being discarded ([#5512](https://github.com/kubestellar/hive/issues/5512)).
### Fixed
- `CreateIssue` now refuses a structurally impossible repository reference before making any GitHub API call. Agent policy prompts show repo arguments as templates (`gh issue create --repo "<org>/<target-repo>"` in `pkg/policies/defaults/sec-check-holdgated.md`), and an agent that copies the line verbatim instead of substituting produced a request naming the literal repo `<org>/<target-repo>`. hive called GitHub with it — `POST .../repos/%3Corg%3E/%3Ctarget-repo%3E/labels: 404` once per label, plus a dedupe lookup and a create — then retried on the watcher's backoff for up to the 24h give-up horizon. The request can never succeed, so every call was waste charged against the App installation's rate limit, the same budget `request_retry.go` records as having taken a whole org dark for an hour at a time. Also rejects `.`/`..` components, which are made of legal characters but traverse the URL rather than name a repository.

- Fixed a v4 issue request watcher test race by joining watcher goroutines before restoring test globals and pinning PR/issue watcher directories per loop.

## 2026-09-05 (v4.17.1)

### Fixed

- Fleet health now counts only the agents actually named by provider-quota telemetry, surfaces pre-threshold start-failure causes in agent verdicts, and renders structured GitHub App auth failures with App/installation/host details ([#6031](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6031), [#6035](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6035), [#6037](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6037)).

## 2026-09-05 (v4.17.0)

### Added

- Fleet health now carries output-freshness telemetry so L3-L6 no-write verdicts explain whether kicks are broken, intentionally idle, advisory-only, budget-suppressed, or queued work is not writable ([#6036](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6036)).

### Fixed

- Spoke health now treats on-demand/off-schedule stopped agents as idle instead of down, and zero-token checks explain the cause while skipping all-paused or no-due hives ([#6030](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6030), [#6032](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6032)).

## 2026-09-05 (v4.16.1)

### Fixed

- Classify Bob API-key 401/expired failures as login/credential problems instead of restart-needed agents.
- Do not default vLLM to a cluster-specific in-cluster Service; report DNS gateway faults with the unresolved host.

### Security

- Stop accepting the shared dashboard owner token from `?token=` URLs; browser terminal links now use authenticated, short-lived single-use handoff codes and query `token`/`code` values are redacted in dashboard request logs.

## 2026-09-05 (v4.16.0)

### Added

- Surface agent restart storms as hosted-fleet problems and add per-agent restart-counter resets from `/fleet`.

## 2026-09-05 (v4.15.0)

### Added

- A new `version-absent` drift signal ("Heartbeat without a version") flags a hive whose heartbeats keep arriving with an empty `git_hash` ([#6025](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6025)). The hub decides whether to instruct an upgrade by comparing the reported hash against the branch target, so a hive that beats without one is never told to upgrade - it freezes at whatever build it happens to run while still counting as online, and no surface an operator looks at tells that apart from health. Two hives sat in this state for hours: one beat every 120s for hours with 0 successful stats collections behind it, sending last-good cached stats with no version and running digest `14c75aa0` while `:stable` was `ef5a603a`; another showed "No heartbeat for 8h29m" and "Upgrading 29m" at the same time because the hub had no fresh version to update the row with. The signal is critical, fires only after three consecutive version-less beats (about six minutes, so a normal spoke start-up cannot trip it), resets the moment a beat carries a version again, and skips offline hives and pool placeholders exactly as the other fleet-relative signals do.

### Changed

- The hub fleet-stats tile strip no longer renders the "Unclaimed" tile. It counted placeholders wedged at `Status=assigned && !ClaimDelivered` - a claim was stamped but the spoke never reported the project back - and drew it in the `warn` style, so it read as an actionable warning while nothing acted on it. It also sat directly beside the `Pool` tile (genuinely free slots), and two tiles both fairly described as "unclaimed" side by side invited misreading. The underlying `assigned_unclaimed` stat, the `AssignedUnclaimed` row field, the admin "Reset assignment" row action and the assign-stuck self-heal sweep are all unchanged; only the tile stops rendering.

### Fixed

- A spoke landing on a node whose kernel lacks the netfilter modules the forced-egress gate needs now says so in one line and exits with the documented preflight code 77, instead of dying at exit 4 on whichever iptables append happened to run first ([#6003](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6003)). `entrypoint.sh` probes the `REDIRECT` target and the packet-mark match in a throwaway chain before it builds `HIVE_PROXY`, and a failure names `xt_REDIRECT` / `xt_mark` explicitly and points at the durable fix - a MachineConfig writing an `/etc/modules-load.d/` drop-in so the modules survive a node rebuild. `xt_owner` stays optional, since OpenShift/OVN runs without it. Behaviour for who runs where is unchanged: such a node still refuses to start, because it cannot enforce egress there. What changed is that the operator is told which module to load. The alternative - guarding the appends with `|| true` and continuing - was rejected: it would boot a spoke with a `HIVE_PROXY` chain that redirects nothing, which is the silent-bypass failure that got `bin/setup-proxy-iptables.sh` deleted in #5949. That is not hypothetical: one spoke survived these same failed appends with a half-built chain and went mute for hours - 104 proxy read timeouts, 34 heartbeat collect timeouts, zero successful collections, no git_hash reported - so the hub never upgraded it while it reported healthy.
- A single invalid per-agent overlay file no longer bricks the whole hive ([#6024](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6024)). A hosted spoke crash-looped for eight hours on `agent supervisor: backend "copilot" contradicts launch_cmd "bob --model auto"`, and the contradiction was not in `hive.yaml` at all - it came from `/data/agent-configs/supervisor.yaml`, which `LoadAgentOverrides` merges over the config after it loads. Because the process exits before the dashboard binds, the documented fix (`PUT /api/config/agent/{name}/...`) was served by the very process the config was killing, so there was no supported way back in short of scaling the Deployment to zero and hand-editing the PVC from a maintenance pod. Invalid overlays are now rejected individually: the offending file is skipped, the agent falls back to its main-config entry so the running roster does not change, the skip is logged at ERROR naming both the agent and the file, and the hive boots with its dashboard reachable.
- Config validation errors now name the file an agent came from - `agent supervisor (from /data/agent-configs/supervisor.yaml): ...` instead of just `agent supervisor: ...` ([#6024](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6024)). `hive.yaml`, the ConfigMap seed, the dashboard overlay and the per-agent overlay directory all feed the same agent map, and naming only the agent sent an operator editing three files that were never the problem.
- New `hive validate` (alias `hive --config-check`) loads the config exactly the way a real boot does - per-agent overlay files included, since that is where the #6024 contradiction lived - prints any error and exits non-zero, without starting anything ([#6024](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/6024)). Previously the only way to learn a config was invalid was to apply it and watch a pod crash-loop.

## 2026-09-05 (v4.14.1)

### Fixed

- The `dibs` cutover preflight no longer reports an unanswered crt.sh query as Let's Encrypt headroom ([#5925](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5925)). crt.sh answers a query it cannot service with `HTTP 200` and a body of `[]` — measured six consecutive times for `hivecommons.dev`, a domain that has a live Let's Encrypt wildcard and ~50 certificates minted the day before — so every guard in the check passed and the parse yielded zero, rendering `✓ 0 certificate(s) in the last 168h; headroom 50`. That was a confident green on the one gate gating the irreversible, quota-spending step, where a 429 costs two slots against the weekly cap rather than one. Zero rows *for the domain at all* is now reported as an unanswered query; zero rows *inside the window*, out of rows actually returned, is still real headroom and still passes. Both cutover scripts also stop reading an unreachable DNS resolver as a wrong or missing A record: `dig +short` prints its `;; communications error …` diagnostics to stdout and signals the failure only through its exit status, so a host with no resolver was told `dibs.hivecommons.dev resolves to ';; communications error …', expected 157.151.252.29` — and the preflight told the operator to create a record the issue had already confirmed exists. Both now report the lookup as unchecked, matching the contract both scripts already stated: a check that could not run is a warning, never a pass.

## 2026-09-04 (v4.14.0)

### Added

- The dibs domain cutover (#5925) gains a post-cutover verifier, `src/deploy/dibs-domain-cutover/verify.sh`, alongside the preflight that already guarded the Let's Encrypt window. The two answer different questions: the preflight asks "is it safe to spend the issuance?", the verifier asks "did it actually work?" — and for this sequence the obvious answer is wrong five separate ways, because every failure mode still returns HTTP 200. A host served ingress-nginx's self-signed default certificate answers 200; a certificate without `dibs.hivecommons.dev` in its SANs answers 200; a redirect Ingress rendered inert by the application Ingress still claiming the same host answers 200; a redirect that drops `$request_uri` passes a bare-root check while breaking every deep link; and a `dibs` that renders every visitor as signed out — the broken state this cutover exists to repair — looks perfectly healthy from outside. So the verifier never accepts a status code as evidence on its own: it reads the issuer and SAN list off the certificate the host actually serves, computes whether the hub session cookie's `Domain` can reach that host at all, tests the redirect on a path *with* a query string, and checks that exactly one Ingress claims the legacy host. It is read-only (DNS lookup, HTTPS GETs, one TLS handshake, `kubectl get`), exits `78` on any failing check, reports a check it could not run as a warning rather than a pass, and treats the open dual-host window as a warning rather than a finish line. Contract tests (`bin/test_dibs_cutover_verify.sh`, 37 cases against stub `openssl`/`curl`/`dig`/`kubectl`) run in CI. Running it against the live hosts also re-measured the cutover's state: the DNS A record the issue recorded as missing now exists, so step 1 is already done.
- The dashboard's **Repositories** section has a **🔄 Rescan** button, next to the one the ACMM Evaluation panel already had. The repo cards — the issue and PR counts and the ticket pills under each repo — are repainted only when the governor's eval cycle runs, and that cadence stretches to minutes on a quiet hive, so an operator who had just closed an issue or opened a PR had no way to ask for a fresh read and was left staring at pills they knew were stale. Pressing Rescan re-enumerates every monitored repository's open issues and pull requests now (`POST /api/repos/rescan`) and republishes the dashboard snapshot. The scan is read-only with respect to the hive: it runs the enumeration half of the eval cycle — issues, PRs, PR CI status, the duplicate-PR claim guard — and none of the acting half, so it never changes the governor's mode, kicks an agent, posts an advisory, or ages a PR toward escalation. It is debounced server-side at 30 seconds and collapses concurrent presses onto a single sweep, so holding the button down cannot spend the hive's GitHub API budget.

### Fixed

- Handle failed non-optional HIVE_PROXY iptables appends explicitly by logging stderr, flushing partial rules, and using the advisory/fail-closed proxy path.
- A failed self-upgrade no longer reports `self-upgrade failed after 5 attempts:` with nothing after the colon. `recordUpgradeError` used to return early when the marker file could not be read, dropping the cause; it now records the error against a fresh marker and warns. When no cause was captured at all, the operator-facing summary says so and names the usual reason - the deployment tracks a tag that does not carry the target SHA - instead of ending in a dangling colon.

## 2026-09-04 (v4.13.5)

### Fixed

- Upgrade targeting is now channel-aware: a spoke whose Deployment tracks a release channel (`:stable`, `:candidate`, `:edge`) is told to reach the commit that channel currently carries, not the newest merged SHA on the release branch ([#5994](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5994)). Since the stable soak policy landed, per-merge publishes move `:candidate` only and `:stable` advances hourly after a 24h soak, so branch HEAD is a digest a `:stable` spoke's own tag is deliberately withholding — the spoke rolled, re-pulled `:stable`, reported the SHA it started on, and the hub re-sent the identical instruction until the retry budget was gone. 41 spokes were in `UPGRADE FAILED` this way, with 90 of 97 tracking `:stable`. The hub now resolves the channel tag to the commit it was built from (the `org.opencontainers.image.revision` label, the same evidence `promote-stable.sh` reads), targets that, and treats a channel spoke sitting at that commit as up to date. When a channel cannot be resolved at all the hub instructs nothing and says so, rather than falling back to a target it knows the spoke cannot reach.

## 2026-09-04 (v4.13.4)

### Fixed

- The stable promotion gate now logs the revision it evaluated and each required workflow's verdict. A hold on "missing green release evidence" previously named only the category, never which workflow on which revision concluded what, so the reason had to be reconstructed by hand from outside the run.

## 2026-09-04 (v4.13.3)

### Fixed

- The stable promotion gate no longer treats a path-filtered workflow as a failure. `docker.yml` publishes a candidate on every push, while `v2-ci.yml` and `v2-tests.yml` only run on pushes touching `src/**`, so a docs-only or workflow-only merge produced a candidate the required suites never looked at - and, because only the newest candidate is evaluated, that blocked promotion until the next code change. Evidence now distinguishes success, failure and did-not-run: when a required workflow did not run on the candidate, the gate inherits the verdict of the nearest ancestor that did run it, stopping at the first ancestor where it ran so a real failure is never inherited past.
- `v2-tests.yml` now also runs on push to `v2` and `v4`. It previously ran only on a schedule and on pull requests, so it landed on whatever SHA happened to be HEAD at :30 and no merged commit carried a `v2-tests` success. The stable promotion gate requires one, so it held on every candidate - including under an emergency exception, which by design refuses to bypass missing green evidence. The push paths filter mirrors the pull_request one.

## 2026-09-04 (v4.13.2)

### Fixed

- The stable promotion gate could never promote. It queried the Actions runs API with the 7-character revision read from the candidate image's label, but that API matches `head_sha` exactly and returns zero runs for a short SHA, so green release evidence was always absent - including under an emergency exception, which by design refuses to bypass missing green evidence. The revision is now expanded to the full 40-character SHA before the query, falling back to the input when it cannot be resolved so an unconfirmable candidate still holds.

## 2026-09-04 (v4.13.1)

### Fixed

- The scheduled v4 test run no longer fails with a data race in `pkg/dashboard` ([#5985](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5985)). `TestPollCopilotToken_SlowDownBumpsInterval` ran the poller in a goroutine it did not own: on a `select` timeout it called `t.Fatalf`, which runs the test's cleanups, and those restore the package-level `copilotAccessTokenURL` / `copilotUserTokenPath` vars that the still-running poller reads. So a timing flake did not fail as a timing flake — it failed as a **DATA RACE** that took the whole `-race` package down, and the abandoned goroutine went on mutating package state under later tests. The margin was thin because the test had to live through the production 5-second slow_down bump in real time with an 8-second budget, which a loaded `-race` shard ate. All seven poll tests now start the poller through a helper that owns it with a cancelable context and joins it in a cleanup registered to run *before* the vars are restored, so the timeout path is no longer a race; the slow_down bump joins the URL and path vars as an injectable `var` so the test proves the same behaviour with a 1-second bump. The test went from 15.6s (failing) to 1.0s, and the package is clean under `-race`.

## 2026-09-04 (v4.13.0)

### Added

- Enforce the v4 stable soak policy in CI: `candidate` continues to advance on every green v4 image build, while `stable` is promoted later by digest only after the soak, evidence, blocker, smoke, and monotonic safety gates pass.

### Changed

- Hosted spokes can now be served by the fleet wildcard certificate instead of minting one certificate per hive ([#5977](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5977)). A wildcard covering `*.<cluster domain>` already existed and served nothing: every provisioned spoke Ingress carried its own `tls:` block, and an explicit `tls:` block always wins over the ingress controller's `--default-ssl-certificate`, so the wildcard was inert while each hive kept issuing its own certificate — measured at ~195 Ingress TLS references and ~60 `Certificate` objects across two clusters on 2026-09-04, against a Let's Encrypt cap of 50 issuances per week per registered domain. With ~263 wildcard-covered hostnames on those clusters that made every hostname change cost a certificate, which is what exhausted the quota during the 2026-09-03 incident. Setting `wildcard_tls_secret` on a cluster in `clusters.json` now makes provisioned spoke Ingresses omit both the per-host `tls:` block and the `cert-manager.io/cluster-issuer` annotation — the first is what beats the default certificate, the second is what makes cert-manager mint the per-host Certificate at all. It is opt-in per cluster and off by default, because omitting the `tls:` block on a cluster whose controller has no `--default-ssl-certificate` makes ingress-nginx serve its built-in **self-signed** certificate and fails TLS on every spoke dashboard at once; the two cluster prerequisites are documented in [Serving spokes from the fleet wildcard certificate](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/blob/v4/src/docs/spoke-wildcard-tls.md). Coverage is decided per host rather than per cluster and follows the RFC 6125 single-label rule, so a host the wildcard cannot cover — the domain apex, a deeper name, or one a level above the wildcard's scope such as `dibs.hivecommons.dev` ([#5925](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5925)) — keeps its own certificate even on an opted-in cluster. OpenShift Route clusters are unaffected: their Routes use edge termination and never minted a per-host certificate through this path.
- Reusable CI workflows now come from `hivecommons/infra` instead of `kubestellar/infra`, completing the org transfer for the last 10 callers. The mirrored copies carry Apache-2.0 attribution to the KubeStellar Authors and swap KubeStellar-specific links for Hive Commons ones; behavior is otherwise unchanged.

## 2026-09-04 (v4.12.6)

### Security

- Bump the transitive `qs` dependency used by dashboard and proxy Node tooling to 6.16.0 to address [GHSA-f9cm-2x4f-4r3g](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-f9cm-2x4f-4r3g) ([#5970](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5970)).

## 2026-09-04 (v4.12.5)

### Fixed

- The `dibs.hivecommons.dev` cutover runbook gained a zero-cost preflight and three sharper verification steps ([#5925](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5925)). The sequence is scheduled around a Let's Encrypt quota window, which makes the expensive failure not "a step fails" but "a step fails *after* the certificate has been re-issued" — that spends the window the whole plan waits for and puts the next attempt a week out. `src/deploy/dibs-domain-cutover/preflight.sh` checks the plan's assumptions read-only and in advance: that the ingress-nginx controller's `--default-ssl-certificate` really is `hive-hub/hive-wildcard-tls` (the staged Ingress gives the new host no `tls:` block and cannot — an Ingress may only name a Secret in its own namespace — so if that flag is absent the host is served ingress-nginx's **self-signed** certificate, answering `200` while failing validation); that the Certificate exists where the patch targets it and does not already carry the SAN; that the live `dibs/dibs` Ingress has not drifted from the staged full-object copy, whose apply would silently drop any annotation it omits; that neither host is claimed by two Ingresses, which ingress-nginx resolves in favour of the older one with only a log line, leaving a redirect inert; that the DNS record points where step 1 says; and that the Let's Encrypt registered-domain window still has headroom, estimated by a read-only crt.sh count rather than any ACME probe. A check it could not run reports as a warning, never as a pass. The runbook now also verifies the certificate chain and SANs rather than just a `200`, states that the legacy host must be removed from the application Ingress *before* the redirect is applied and why, tests the redirect on a deep path with a query so one that drops `$request_uri` cannot pass a `/`-only check, and names the dual-host window as a verification window rather than a resting state — the legacy host is knowingly signed-out for as long as it stays open. Contract tests: `bin/test_dibs_cutover_preflight.sh`.
- Agents that cannot start now say why, and the hive stops relaunching them forever ([#5958](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5958), from [#5921](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5921)). A hive spent 4,025 log lines and a fresh tmux session every ~3 minutes relaunching agents that could not possibly come up: Copilot was not logged in, a bob API key was rejected, and one agent was configured `backend: copilot` but launched by `launch_cmd: bob` — so the readiness probe waited for a prompt the launched binary never prints. All three are ordinary, user-fixable conditions, and none of them reached the operator: the card, the fleet row and the log all said `restart needed` or `hung`, and the fleet page showed the agents green and "able" the whole time. A failed start is now recorded with a stable class and an actionable reason — `copilot: not logged in`, `bob: API key rejected (401)`, `backend/launch_cmd mismatch` — which rides agent state out to the dashboard card, the heartbeat and the fleet verdict. Consecutive failures of the SAME reason escalate a backoff (1m → 5m → 15m → 30m) and, after three, mark the agent blocked: the crash-restart loop and the Copilot hang diagnostic both stand down, and the fleet's `able` count excludes it, so a hive can no longer report agents as able when none of them started. The block is a pause, not a dead end — the 30-minute ceiling keeps re-probing (the fixes here happen out of band, with nothing to notify the hive that a human logged in or pasted a key), and an explicit retry — the dashboard restart button, or the relaunch that follows saving a bob key — clears the backoff outright rather than making an operator wait. The Copilot hang check also now keys on the agent's effective backend instead of its declared one, and reports a `backend`/`launch_cmd` contradiction by name instead of running a diagnostic that cannot converge.

## 2026-09-04 (v4.12.4)

### Fixed

- Hub latest-image status now marks long-unavailable builds as stale/unknown and links failed Docker runs instead of spinning forever.

## 2026-09-04 (v4.12.3)

### Changed

- Operators now have a staged `dibs.hivecommons.dev` cutover runbook and manifests for the delayed Certificate SAN update, dual-host Ingress, and legacy `dibs.kubestellar.io` redirect, preserving the Let's Encrypt quota hold and manual DNS gate from [#5925](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5925).
- Documented the hosted Hive Hub cutover to `https://hive.hivecommons.dev`, including legacy redirect guidance and hosted-hub onboarding.

### Fixed

- The advisory digest's top-N no longer spends its scarce slots on findings it then tells the reader not to trust ([#2364](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/2364)). The renderer captions a finding as not-current for three reasons — its file is gone at the analyzed commit ([#3704](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/3704)), its evidence was computed at some other commit ([#5130](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5130)), or it was republished from byte-identical cached text ([#5236](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5236)) — but only the first one influenced which findings won a slot, and provenance staleness was not even computed until after the cap had already chosen. So a finding nothing had re-checked could displace one that WAS confirmed at the commit the digest is stamped with. The live 2026-09-04 digest showed the cost: of the ten findings it rendered out of 302, three carried an outdated or unverified caption, including two of the four CRITICAL slots. Ranking now demotes on all three signals alike. It stays a demotion, not a filter: unverified means nobody re-checked the finding, not that it is fixed, so an unverified finding still backfills any slot no confirmed finding of equal severity claims, still keeps its caption when it does, and an unverified critical still outranks a confirmed low — severity continues to dominate.
- Documented the constraint that makes a public-hostname move an ordered operation rather than a rename, and pinned it in the hub test suite ([#5925](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5925)). First-party sibling products have no login of their own: `dibs` resolves a visitor by forwarding the browser's `hive_hub_user` cookie to the hub's `/api/saas/whoami` ([#4171](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/4171)), which works only while the browser sends that cookie to the sibling — and it does so solely because the cookie is scoped to the registrable domain of the hub's own canonical host. The hub and the sibling must therefore share a registrable domain. When the hub became `hive.hivecommons.dev` on 2026-09-04 the session cookie became `Domain=.hivecommons.dev`, and `dibs.kubestellar.io` stopped receiving it; the symptom is not an error but a sibling that renders every visitor as signed out. Moving `dibs` under `hivecommons.dev` is what repairs it. Operators get a new **Moving a public hostname** runbook in the migration tracker covering that coupling, the Let's Encrypt issuance cap the re-issue has to wait for, and the wildcard's actual coverage (`*.hive.hivecommons.dev` does **not** cover a name one level up such as `dibs.hivecommons.dev`); the `HIVE_HUB_PUBLIC_URL` reference now warns that changing its registrable domain signs users out of siblings left on the old one. The runbook also records why the retired host must serve a redirect instead of continuing to serve the application: a browser ignores a `Set-Cookie` whose `Domain` does not cover the host that sent it, so a hub on `hivecommons.dev` can never scope a session to `kubestellar.io`, and no configuration exists that would let it — the redirect is the mechanism, not a preference.
- Hosted hive vanity URL repair now persists its success cooldown and fleet mint ledger, backs off failed repair attempts, and exposes env-tunable limits so a stale-route storm cannot exhaust Let’s Encrypt certificate quota again ([#5923](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5923)).
- Agent configuration now rejects clear backend/launch_cmd contradictions at load and Settings-save time, preventing agents from launching as one CLI while being diagnosed as another ([#5921](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5921)).

### Security

- Removed `bin/setup-proxy-iptables.sh` and its `bin/README.md` "Enforcement" listing ([#5948](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5948)). The script was dead code — no Dockerfile, systemd unit, or entrypoint invoked it — yet it advertised itself as making the ACMM proxy "un-bypassable". Its gate was trivially bypassable (it only redirected the IPv4 addresses `dig` returned at startup, GitHub rotates IPs, and it never touched IPv6), fail-open (every rule append ended in `|| true` and a missing `dig` produced zero rules while still reporting success), and its root-only owner exemption would have looped a non-root proxy's own upstream dials back into itself. The maintained, fail-closed enforcement lives in `src/deploy/entrypoint.sh` (`HIVE_PROXY` chain with proxy-UID + packet-mark exemptions and an IPv6 REJECT gate); keeping a second, weaker copy in `bin/` invited operators to deploy an egress gate that only pretended to contain the write path.

## 2026-09-04 (v4.12.2)

### Fixed

- Fleet health now attributes silent agents to failing model gateways before blaming the GitHub App: spokes report per-gateway DNS/connect/5xx/auth/budget failures from runtime proxy traffic and Gateway Test, agents on failed gateways no longer count as able, and the fleet hint points operators to Settings → Model Gateways ([#5914](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5914), [#5917](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5917)).
- Inference-backed agents now classify provider API failures before the anti-narration watchdog fires, back off retries, and show the blocked inference error in the dashboard instead of looping on “execute it yourself” nudges ([#5915](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5915)).
- The hub fleet row now uses one advisory-digest freshness calculation for both the right-side freshness chip and stale verdict, so a hive cannot show `advisories fresh` and `advisory stale` in the same render; rows without an advisory signal render `n/a` instead ([#5916](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5916)).

## 2026-09-04 (v4.12.1)

### Fixed

- The release changelog mover now anchors on the real `## Unreleased` heading line instead of the first prose mention, and this repair restores the stranded entries for the affected v4 releases ([#5891](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5891)).

## 2026-09-04 (v4.12.0)

### Fixed

- The advisory digest's **Recently Resolved** changelog now honours `governor.advisory.max_findings` instead of rendering up to 100 healed findings regardless ([#2364](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/2364)). The cap bounded only the open findings, so the section a reader opens the digest *for* became the small part of it: the live 2026-09-03 digest rendered 10 open findings in 4,937 characters — under a note saying 286 more existed — and 100 already-fixed ones in 22,138, making 82% of the comment work that was already done. Withheld resolved entries are now announced the way withheld findings are (*"…plus 20 more resolved in the last 48h"*), `show_all: true` lifts the cap off the changelog as well, and the 100-entry ceiling that keeps the comment inside GitHub's character limit still applies above it — stated now rather than trimming in silence.
- The backend smoke's pinned lane runs again, and a runner fault no longer gets reported as broken contributor CLIs ([#5883](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5883)). Moving the workflow to the self-hosted fleet ([#5862](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/pull/5862)) kept the image-heavy jobs on hosted runners but missed that the pinned lane is image-heavy by definition — it runs the suite *inside* `ghcr.io/hivecommons/hive-contributor`, so it needs podman, and the fleet image has none. Every scheduled run died at the image pull in about seven seconds with `podman: command not found`, so the CLI versions hive ships have been unverified since. The pinned lane now runs on `ubuntu-latest` while the latest lane stays on the fleet, keeping the queue relief that motivated the move; a preflight step names a missing container engine outright instead of exiting 127, and writes that into the evidence artifact so it is not empty. The failure-issue job also stopped guessing: it filed "[Backend smoke: pinned] … the CLI versions hive SHIPS in the contributor image failed the smoke — contributors are likely broken right now" about a suite that had never executed, which is a false accusation aimed at the wrong owner. It now checks whether the suite step actually ran, reports a pre-suite failure as an environment fault naming the step that broke, and threads those separately from real CLI failures — the same rule the lane split already follows, since "the shipped CLIs are broken" and "the runner could not start the suite" have different owners and different urgency. That script is JavaScript embedded in a workflow that only executes on a scheduled failure in production, so it had no coverage at all; `bin/test_backend_smoke_attribution.sh` now extracts it from the workflow and runs it against fixtures built from the real failing run.
- ACMM's `acmm:prereq-code-style` criterion now recognizes explicit formatter and linter configuration beyond the JS/Python/Go files it already accepted, including shell/editor style (`.shellcheckrc`, `.editorconfig`), C/C++ (`.clang-format`), Java Checkstyle (`checkstyle.xml`, `config/checkstyle/checkstyle.xml`), Ruby RuboCop, Rust rustfmt/clippy, SwiftLint, Stylelint, and additional golangci-lint config extensions ([#5903](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5903)). Because this is an L0 prerequisite, repositories written in those languages were pinned below Level 0 and could see the same gap issue re-filed after adding a valid style config. Broad manifests such as `package.json`, `go.mod`, `Cargo.toml`, or `Makefile` are still not accepted, so the criterion does not become a trivial match for repos without code-style configuration.

## 2026-09-03 (v4.11.0)

### Fixed

- A user already signed in to the hub who is bounced to `/login?redirect=…` (for example by a spoke's auth gate) is now returned straight to the validated target instead of being shown the login provider picker and forced through OAuth again.

## 2026-09-03 (v4.10.0)

### Added

- The ACMM Evaluation panel gained a **🔄 Re-evaluate** button, and `GET /api/acmm/evaluation?refresh=1` bypasses the hour-long result cache ([#5877](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5877)). The panel drives a fix-and-verify loop — read the failing criteria, commit the missing file, confirm the criterion flips — but the evaluation was cached in memory for an hour with no invalidation and no refresh, so the confirmation half was unavailable short of restarting the dashboard, and nothing said the numbers were stale. The forced refresh is debounced server-side (a refresh within a minute of the last evaluation returns the cached result), so the button cannot spend the GitHub API budget, and the `Evaluated:` line now renders as a relative age ("Evaluated 43m ago", absolute timestamp in the tooltip) so staleness is legible even without clicking anything. Without `refresh=1` the endpoint behaves exactly as before.

### Fixed

- Stopped hub provisioning from falling back to a machine's ambient kubeconfig. A cluster config claiming `in_cluster` outside a real pod now aims kubectl at the unreachable sentinel and fails loudly, instead of applying namespaces and Deployments to whatever cluster the current context named.
- The dashboard's ACMM Evaluation panel now shows which repo is lagging, instead of one fleet-wide level that hid it ([#5822](https://github.com/kubestellar/hive/issues/5822)). The headline number is a union across watched repos — a criterion counts as passed if *any* repo has it — so a repo meeting none of the 43 criteria did not move it at all and was effectively invisible; and the per-repo drill-down was half-wired, so clicking a repo chip moved the level bars while the four headline tiles above them kept reporting hive-wide numbers, two halves of one card disagreeing with nothing to say which had followed the click. The tiles now resolve the selected repo *before* they are written, Overall ACMM is recomputed as min(selected repo's codebase level, operational level) rather than silently keeping the fleet-wide reading, and Operational Autonomy — which measures the hive and not a repository — stays hive-wide and is now labelled as such, so the card's mixed scope is stated rather than left looking like a tile that failed to update. A new per-repo table ranks every watched repo weakest-first by unmet-criteria count, showing its level, `passed/total`, and the level it is blocked at, with rows that select their repo; the chips carry the ratio alongside the level, so "lacking, and by how much" no longer costs one click per repo. The panel itself now states that the aggregate is a union — previously that was mentioned only inside the Codebase Readiness dialog — and the repo selector no longer flashes visible before the first fetch, having carried both `display:none` and `display:flex` in one style attribute. No new data collection or GitHub API calls: the evaluation already returned per-repo results.
- The ACMM panel's bulk **Open All** now reports WHY issue creation failed instead of a bare count followed by template text about the `acmm` + `ai-fix-requested` labels ([#5873](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5873)). The server has always returned the underlying GitHub error in the response body — `410 Issues has been disabled in this repository`, a `403`, a `422` — and the single-criterion Open Issue button has always shown it; the batch path discarded the body unread, collapsed every failure mode into `failed++`, and then printed the label note unconditionally, which reads as a diagnosis and pointed operators at labels that were never the problem. Failures are now collected per repo:criterion, deduplicated by reason, and shown in the summary dialog exactly as the single path shows them; the label note appears only when something was actually created, since it describes what a successful create did.
- The Tagged Release workflow's "Open and merge a PR" step now honors an optional `RELEASE_PR_TOKEN` secret, falling back to `GITHUB_TOKEN` when unset ([#5875](https://github.com/hivecommons/hive/issues/5875)).
